yderville Basin Planning Commission

Proposal 
The applicant is proposing an amendment to the Canyons Specially Planned Area  Amended 
and Restated Development Agreement to modify Section 3.15 “Automobile Prohibition at Red 
Pine Village, Tombstone, and Red Pine Lake Development Areas." That applica nts propose to 
add the following language: In general, automobile access to these areas other than for 
residents and guest s by gate-c ontrolled non-public road will be prohibited.” EXHIBIT A 
Project Description: 
P
roject Name: Canyons Development Agreement Amendment  to Section 
3.15 - Automobile Access to Red Pine Village , Tombstone 
and Red Pine Lake Development Areas 
Project #: 23-064
Applicant(s):  Dave Smith, authorized representative
Property Owner(s): TCFC PC Leaseco, LC

Vicinity Map: 
 
Background: 
 
The Canyons Specially Planned Area (SPA) was originally approved in 1998.  This approval 
involved 6,388 acres  and five (5) different property owners.  With that approval there were 
entitlements granted for some of the developments at the Canyons known as Ski 98.  This 
included developments such as the Grand Summit and Sundial Lodge, the Colony and the 
Silverado.   It was anticipated that there would be a comprehensive amendment to this plan 
that would expand the entitlements and property involved. 
  
In November of 1999, that amendment to the SPA was completed.  After numerous public 
meetings and hearings, the SPA was extended to include approximately 7,768 acres and 37 
different property owners to establish the current Development Agreement (DA).  The guiding 
principles for the DA were the Global Principles: 
 
• Comfortable Carrying Capacity 
4
3 
• Allowable Density
• Unit Configurations & Occupancy
• Resort Support and Mountain Recreation
• Viewshed
• Environment
• Employee Housing
• Economic Base
• Transportation
• Highway 224 & Resort Entry
• Benchmark Assessments
• Development Design Character
• Master Community and Resort Facility, Amenity, Recreation, Cultural Arts, and
Marketing Plan
• Community Integration
• Infrastructure Maintenance and Management
• Construction Mitigation and Management
• Development Pattern
• Development Phasing
T
he key components to the Development Agreement (DA) were to establish development  
planning areas, create a detailed Land Use & Zoning Chart to allocate maximum heights, 
maximum square footages, uses for each parcel, create Comprehensive Design Guidelines, set 
benchmarks for a Canyons Transportation Master, the Canyons Workforce Housing Plan, Trials 
Master Plan, Natural Resources Management Plan and Open Space and Viewshed Protection 
Plan, to name a few.   
T
here are twelve (12) distinct development areas within the SPA. Each development area has 
specific planning standards based on its location, including allowable uses, density, and 
configuration, as outlined in the Development Agreement and its accompanying exhibits.  
Due
 to the location and phasing requirements outlined in the Development Agreement, the 
mid-mountain development has not commenced and cannot begin until specific  thresholds are 
met. As part of the long -range planning for the Red Pine Village, Red Pine Lake, and Tombstone 
development areas, the applicant is working to address access and traffic concerns for these 
areas. 
S
ection 3.15 states: “Automobile Prohibition at Red Pine Village, Tombstone, and Red Pine Lake 
Development Areas.  As required in Section 3.3.5(a) of this Amended Agreement, a remote 
parking and management plan, to include check -in, concierge service and transportation 
between parking and accommodation, must be included in the Transportation Plan. This aspect 
of the Plan shall be adopted and implemented in conjunction with hotel lodging unit 
development in Red Pine Village, Tombstone, and Red Pine Lake Development Areas. In general, 
automobile access to these areas will be prohibited. Access will be provided and maintained for 
service vehicles and emergency services.” 
5
4 
Staff has included additiona l exhibits from the DA that address the  site-specific design that 
also speak to the limitation of automobiles within these development areas. EXHIBIT B 
Per their application statement, TCFC has vetted Red Pine traffic -related matters with County 
personnel and the Fire Marshal, including access for service vehicles and emergency services . 
From those discussions, it has become clear that if the "general" limitation under Section 3.15 
were to be applied in such a manner as to strictly prohibit all vehicular access not only for the 
general public but for residents and guests in particular, such a strict prohibition would 
eliminate a crucial means of evacuation that must be available in the event of a wildfire or 
other life-threatening emergency. The Fire Mashal has provided an updated letter that states 
(i) the use of a gondola is not a sufficient means of egress for occupants of the development 
and does not meet the requirements of the Park City Fire District or the International Fire Code,  
and likewise, (ii) the use of shuttles is not a sufficient option for emergency egress. The Fire 
Marshal has further concluded that, given the constraints of the development area, personal 
automobiles need to be available, as a combination of shuttles and lifts or a gondola would not 
provide a successful means of emergency evacuation required for the numbers and locations of 
people on the mountain. A  copy of (i) the  proposed 11-word redline amendment, (ii) a  letter 
stating that TCFC would be willing to provide, agreeing to the imposition of a requirement that 
the roadway be non-public, gate-controlled a
ccess, and (iii) the Life Safety letter from the Park 
City District Fire Marshal.
T
CFC believes this critical life -safety issue can be adequately addressed and reconciled with the 
general traffic limitation by adding a clarifying phrase in Section 3.15. This phrase would restrict 
vehicular traffic to residents and guests only via a gate -controlled non -public access road. 
Emergency and resort service vehicles would, of course, still be allowed. This approach would 
achieve the dual County goals of (i) reducing traffic and (ii) ensuring life safety by providing the 
only practical means of emergency evacuation. 
I
n September 2023, the SBPC held a work session and requested that the applicant collaborate 
with staff to provide additional information regarding the potential location of the road and its 
possible visual and environmental impacts.   
On July 8, 2025, the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission held a work session to discuss the 
potential amendment. Th e applicant provided  supplemental information, specifically a visual 
analysis and details on construction measures for grading, retaining walls, and bridges.  
Due to technical issues, the discussion time was limited at the July 2025 meeting, so a follow up 
work session was conducted on September 9, 2025. The applicant asked the Park City Fire 
Chief, Mike Owens, to attend in person to help answer questions regarding the necessity of 
vehicular access to the development area and provided  supplemental drawings of the potential 
alignment of High Mountain Road.   
6
5 
 
Prior to moving the application forward for a  public hearing, the applicants have asked for a 
final work session to address  and discuss the Planning Commissions outstanding concerns.  
Exhibit A  provides additional narrative for the proposed amend ment, an updated l etter from 
the PCFD Fire Marshal, illustration of the potential road alignment, including aerial overlays.  In 
addition to an overall contextual depiction, these illustrations include photos taken of existing 
Colony roadway walls and features, which show exactly where they are located within the 
Colony, and then pinpoint where analogous features would be found on the potential roadway.    
 
Recommendation 
 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission conduct a work session and 
discuss the proposed amendment to modify Section 3.15 of the Canyons Development 
Agreement regarding Automobile Prohibition for Red Pine Village, Red Pine Lake , and 
Tombstone Development Areas.   
 
Public Notice, Meetings and Comments 
  
This item has been noticed as a work session only.  After receiving input and direction  from the 
SBPC, staff will schedule the item for a public hearing and possible recommendation at a later 
date.

WHAT’S CHANGED SINCE 1999 
 
1. The Colony has grown up around Red Pine and is fully 
platted, changing the context and nature of mid-
mountain 
2. As of this year, Red Pine is now served by not just one but 
two gondolas – Red Pine and recently completed Sunrise 
3. The physical traffic mitigation features necessary to 
accommodate the Red Pine density have been put in 
place, and other major traffic mitigation measures have 
been implemented 
4. All traffic coming to the Resort will obviously and in any 
event come to the entrance to the Resort, and this year 
the existing surface parking areas internal to the resort 
will be consolidated into the new parking garage, which 
will thereby eliminate substantial internal traffic that 
would otherwise be headed up the hill 
5. There will in any event be a paved road to Red Pine, which 
economics dictate will be constructed in the least 
impactful way possible.  More importantly, the roadway 
alignment has been re-engineered and relocated to a far 
less visually impactful location 
6. Rideshare / CV Connect are in place and well used

SPECIFIC AND OVERALL TRAFFIC MITIGATION 
 
The Traffic Study for the Major 2018 SPA Amendment Accounted for Red Pine: 
This study addresses the traffic impacts associated with the current and proposed 
future development of the Canyons Resort.  It reviews the current traffic and the 
proposed traffic generated at full build-out of the Resort.  This also includes the future 
traffic for Red Pine Village development. 
 
Traffic Mitigation Measures COMPLETED for Full Build-out of the Resort Include: 
- Roundabout at the Canyons Resort Drive / High Mountain Road intersection 
improved to allow free-flowing movements 
- Length of left-turn lanes on eastbound approach to the SR-224 / Canyons 
Resort Drive intersection maximized to increase queuing capacity 
- Canyons Resort Drive widened to five lanes (two lanes in each direction and 
a center turn lane) between SR-224 and Frostwood Drive 
- Canyons Resort Drive restriped for longer queuing lanes and two left-turn 
lanes to the eastbound approach to the SR-224 / Canyons Resort Drive 
Intersection  
- Additional left-turn lane for ‘Bus Only’ to eastbound approach to SR-224 / 
Canyons Resort Drive Intersection, and receiving lane to northbound SR-224 
- Capacity of Canyons Resort Drive / Frostwood Drive intersection 
Increased 
o  Existing roundabout converted from one-lane to a two-lane 
roundabout 
o Road widened after roundabout for two lanes westbound to Resort 
Core 
o Road widened and eastbound lane approaching roundabout changed 
to two lanes 
o Lower Village Road widened and northbound lane approaching 
roundabout changed to two lanes 
- Canyons Resort Drive eastbound and westbound lanes improved for 
transit stop areas outside of travel lanes.

the SP A boundary that have agreed to restrict the development of such property in exchange
for a transfer of density to within the SP A boundary hereby agree to record a covenant
against their property acknowledging the nature and extent of such restrictions.

development areas, any connection to other ski resorts must be approved by both Summit 
County, the Master Developer and the Developer of The Colony. 
Section 3.14 Fractional Interest and Timeshare Unit Conversion Prohibition to Primary 
Residential Units. The parties understand and agree that all timeshare and fractional interest 
units shall be restricted in such a way as to prohibit conversion of such to use as primary 
residential dwelling units. Densities received by Developers for such units are granted by 
the County under the express understanding that these timeshare and fractional interest units 
are for resort accommodation only. 
Section 3.15 Automobile Prohibition at Red Pine Village. Tombstone. and Red Pine Lake 
Development Areas. As required in Section 3.3.5(a) of this Amended Agreement, a remote 
parking and management plan, to include check-in, concierge service and transportation 
between parking and accommodation, must be included in the Transportation Plan. This 
aspect of the Plan shall be adopted and implemented in conjunction with\hotel/lodging unit 
development in Red Pine Village, Tombstone, and Red Pine Lake Development Areas. In 
general, automobile access to these areas will be prohibited. Access will be provided and 
maintained for service vehicles and emergency services.

Proposal 
T
he applicant is requesting t he Snyderville Basin Planning Commission discuss the proposed  
Vintage on the Strand Phase 3 Final Site Plan and Condominium Plat  and make a positive 
recommendation to the County Manager,  EXHIBIT A . The project is located at  2 673 High 
Mountain Road on a parcel known on the Canyons Land Use and Zoning chart as Friedman2/F2. 
The proposed project consists of 10 condominium lodging units accounting for  51,006 square 
feet. The project also includes a 910 square foot manager’s unit /rental office.  T he Canyons 
Development Agreement allocates the Friedman2/F2 parcel 52, 800 square feet of 
Hotel/Lodging use with a maximum of 2-3 stories in height. 
P
roject Description

Background 
 
The Vintage on the Strand Phase 1 Final Site Plan and Condominium Plat was approved in 2005.  
The project is located on land identified in the Canyons SPA land use and zoning chart as 
Freidman1/F1, which is designated for 67,200 square feet of hotel/lodging uses.  Vintage on the 
Strand Phase 1 was approved for 66,374 square feet.  The approved project consisted of multi -
family, townhomes, and single -family dwellings.  It was presented to the Board of County 
Commissioners (BCC) for construction in 4 phases; however, only the first phase was approved, 
recorded, and constructed. During the review process with the BCC, Commissioner Richer was 
concerned about the use of multi -family and single -family homes on a parcel designated for 
hotel/lodging use, and that this use  was contrary to the original vision of the Canyons. Planner 
Barille indicated that some residential and hotel/lodging density was originally allotted to the 
site. However, there is no evidence or documentation to support that assertion.  Commissioner 
Richer conditioned the approval that the units would not qualify for primary residency tax 
exemption and that a rental pool would be made available for units not occupied by the 
primary owner, 
 
In 2008 Vintage on the Strand Phase 2 was recorded.  The purpose was to condominiumize 
building 9 and 9A , which were  approved in Phase 1.  No additional density was approved and 
was consistent with the Phase 1 Final Site Plan approval. 
33
3 
 
 
In July 2015, t he Vintage on the Strand Phase 3 Final Site Plan and Condominium Plat 
application was submitted .  As part of the review process, the staff identified that the 
proposed use was not consistent with the Land Use and Zoning chart. The Canyons 
Development Agreement defines Hotel/Lodging as follows: 
 
"Hotel/Lodging Unit(s)" means a unit which shall contain attributes of a hotel of facility 
established for similar purposes and which shall be available for short term occupancy by 
the unit owner or others.   Attributes shall include: 
 
Central reservation service for all units, including central check-in with full-time front desk 
service, bellhops, and concierge, operated by the owner/operator,  a property management 
company chosen by the owners' association, or as a function of the owner's association; 
 
Central access to the building, with no private entrances for individual units or wings, except 
in structures which include up to but not to exceed four dwelling units, unless otherwise 
approved by the Director; 
 
Pedestrian traffic funneled through a central lobby area, except in structures which include up 
to but not to exceed four dwelling units, unless otherwise approved by the Director; 
 
Centralized parking, with no assigned spaces, except in structures which include up to but not 
to exceed four dwelling units, unless otherwise approved by the Director; 
 
Utilities centrally controlled, including cable television, telephone, electricity, gas, and water; 
and 
 
Limited storage area for owners. 
 
Based on this definition, it was determined that the use did  not meet the above-cited 
hotel/lodging unit definition and therefore d id not comply with the Land Use and Zoning Chart.  
Additionally, Staff has not found any formal amendments to the land use and zoning chart that 
would allow for multi -family and single-family dwelling units on the Friedman/F2 parcel.   Then 
Community Development Director, Pat Putt, met with the applicant and their legal counsel, and 
directions were given to the applicant to propose an amendment to the land use and zoning 
chart or modify the proposal to be consistent with the Hotel/Lodging designation. 
 
The applicant’s legal counsel has provided a letter indicating their position that they are 
“vested” for the multi -family/single-family use.  The legal department  reviewed the letter and 
did not believe that Phase 3 is vested for that use and supported the staff’s recommendation.  
 
The Canyon’s Design Review Committee (DRC) conducted a detailed three -phase review 
process for the Vintage on the Strand Phase 3 Finals Site Plan and Condominium Plat and 
forwarded a positive recommendation, with conditions, on May 25, 2015.    Although the DRC 
found that the project was consistent with the pervious Vintage Phase 1 approvals, it concluded 
34
4 
 
that it was not consistent with the Canyons Land Use and Zoning Chart.  There were several 
conditions required by the DRC, specifically condition 2 states “Approval of an amendment to 
the Land Use and Zoning Chart to adjust the 'Principle Use' or acceptable documentation in 
compliance with the SPA agreements, amendment, and guidelines.  
 
On December 8, 2015, the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission discussed the proposed 
Vintage on the Strand Phase 3 Final Site Plan. Staff recommended that the SBPC direct the 
applicant to apply for an amendment to the Canyons SPA Land Use and Zoning Chart to try 
modify the chart to be consistent with single and multi-family residential use or make a decision 
based on the merits of the applicant and recommend denial of the proposal.  The SBPC 
unanimously recommended denial of the project as proposed to the County Manager. 
 
Following that meeting, the applicants requested an advisory opinion from the State 
Ombudsman.  Following this request, the County Attorney filed two separate responses to the 
State Ombudsman, dated April 19, 2016, and July 19, 2016, . 
 
On November 21, 2016, Staff received an email from authorized representative, Sam Ferrin, 
with a copy of said advisory opinion, EXHIBIT B. The opinion dated November 9, 2016, primarily 
states that the County cannot restrict the appearance of a single family “ structure”, so long as 
the “Use” must comply with the  hotel/lodging definition. After reviewing this opinion, Dave 
Thomas, Deputy County Attorney, responded on November 29, 2016, and requested that the 
applicant provide an updated plan and explanation of how it would meet the definition of 
hotel/lodging outlined in the Canyons DA .   
 
On April 11, 2017, the staff received a formal 45 -day request for action letter filed by the 
property owner with the County Attorney.  The request was for a decision based on the formal 
recommendation from the SBPC, rendered on December 8, 2015. 
 
The County Manager conducted a public hearing on May 4, 2017.  The primary focus of that 
discussion was how the project met the criteria outlined in the Canyons Land Use and Zoning 
chart, which defines Hotel/Lodging .   The two primary areas that needed further clarification 
were how the project would address the “central reservation and check -in” and “centrally 
controlled utilities” criteria.  The applicant was instructed to further address these issues. 
 
On October 23, 2017, the County Manager continued the discussion of the proposal. The 
applicants proposed converting an existing dumpster building located on Lot 9A of the Vintage 
Phase 2 into a Manager’s Unit/Rental office. This would include “Central reservation service for 
all participating units, including central check -in with full-time front desk service, bellhops, and 
concierge, operated by a property management company chosen by the owners’ association, or 
as a function of the owner’s association.” The applicant has provided correspondence from 
Questar Gas (Dominion Energy), Rocky Mountain Power, and Summit Water regarding utility 
connectivity. All three have indicated that the use of remote (single) meters is discouraged or 
35
5 
prohibited. Ultimately, the C ounty Manager issued Finding o f Fact an d Conclusions of La w on 
October 26, 2017, approving the Vintage on the Strand Phase 3 Final Site Plan, EXHIBIT C. 
In March 2019, the applicants reached out to finalize the approvals of the Final Site Plan mylars. 
However, based on 10-9-11 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code, “All other development 
permits shall be considered void after one year unless substantial construction or development 
has taken place or has continued in good faith without interruption; provided, however, that a 
longer period of time may be provided for a phased subdivision plat or phased site plan as set 
forth in a condition attached to a specially planned area plan (development agreement). One 6 -
month extension of a development permit may be granted by the CDD upon his finding that 
special circumstances exist which warrant such an extension, including, but not limited to, a 
delay caused by a government review agency or a natural disaster. (Ord. 708, 12 -10-2008)”.  
Therefore, the October 2017 approval was considered expired and the approvals void. 
On November 12, 2025, the current Final Site Plan a
nd Condominium Plat application was 
received for the Vintage on The Strand Phase 3. The applicants again are proposing to construct 
a Manager’s Unit/Rental office and to control the utilities through the HOA in order to meet the 
standards required for Hotel/Loding units outlined in the Canyons DA, EXHIBIT D.   
The Canyons DRC submitted a let ter of recommendation dated October 19, 2025,  for 
the proposed Vintage on the Sta nd Phase 3 Final Site Plan and Condominium plat. 
Analysis and Findings 
The approval process for all plats and final site plans within the Canyons SPA area, including the 
subject parcels, is governed by t he Canyons SPA Development Agreement.  Section 2.7 of t he 
Amended and Restated Development Agreement, adopted on November 1 5th, 1999, provides 
the Development  A pproval Process f or all Final Su bdivision Plats or C ondominium P lats a nd 
Final Site Plans within the SPA.  After reviewing the proposed Fi nal Site Plan and Condominium 
Plat, the P lanning C ommission s hall make a recommendation t o the B oard of C ounty 
Commissioners.  This a dministrative function is no w handled by t he C ounty Ma nager.  The 
County Manager will t hen c onduct a public hearing an d mak e a  fina l decision of a pproval, 
approval with conditions, or denial of the project. 
Staff’s review of the pet ition, based upon the information presented and with the 
recommendation from the Canyons DRC, indicates that the project generally complies  with the 
Canyons Development Agreement and meet s the minimum requirement for approval in the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code.

F
indings of Fact: 
1. According to the current assessor’s parcel information, Summit County is the owner of
record for parcels VOS-1-2A AND VOS-1-2B.
2. Parcel VOS-1-2A AND VOS -1-2B is zoned Specially Planned Area (SPA) and is within the
Canyons Specially Planned Area (SPA) boundaries.
3. Parcel VOS-1-2A AND VOS-1-2B is intended to be utilized for Hotel/Lodging use , per the
Amended and Restated Canyons Development Agreement Land Use and Zoning Chart.
4. On October 26, 2017, Findings of Fact and Conclusions of Law were approved by the
County Manager for the Vintage on the Strand Phase 3 Site Plan and Condominium Plat.
5. No action was taken to record the Vintage on the Strand Phase 3 Final Site Plan and
Condominium Plat on or before October 26, 2018, therefore, the October 26, 2017,
approval was considered expired and the approvals void.
6. A reapplication for Vintage on the Stand Phase 3  Final Site Plan  and Condominium Plat
was submitted on November 15, 2025.
7. The applicant is proposing to construct ten  ( 10) condominium units to be used for
Hotel/Lodging use.
8. The applicant proposes  to construct a manager’s unit /rental office  to facilitate check -
in/check-out for guests.
9. The parcel is allowed to have structures that are a maximum of 2- 3 stories in height as
described in the Amended and Restated Canyons Development Agreement Land Use
and Zoning Chart, more specifically outlined in the site-specific design guidelines.
10. The Canyons DA requires 0.8 spaces per unit, requiring 8 spaces.  The applicant
proposes a total of 20 parking spaces, two for each unit in an enclosed garage.
11. The Canyons DRC made a formal recommendation for the Vintage on the Stand Phase 3
Final Site Plan on October 19, 2025.
Co
nclusions of Law: 
1. T
he proposed Vintage on the Strand Pha se 3 Final Site Plan and Condominium Plat
complies with all requirements of the Snyderville Basin Development Code and the
amended and restated Canyons Development Agreement.
2. The proposed use, as conditioned, is consistent with the Snyderville Basin General Plan,
as amended.
3. The use is not detrimental to public health, safety and welfare, as the roads and public
services in the area are sufficient to accommodate the increase in intensity of the use.
4. The use is compatible with the existing neighborhood character and will not adversely
affect surrounding land uses.

Conditions of Approval: 
1. The applicant shall amend the Condominiums at Vintage Phase 2  condominium plat to
accommodate the Manager's unit/rental office.  The 910 square feet of the unit shall be
accounted for as part of the density allotted to the Friedman/F2 parcel.
2. A note shall be placed on the Vintage on the Strand Phase 3 Final Site Plan and
Condominium Plat stating that “The principle use shall be as Hotel/Lodging units, and as
such are not eligible for the primary residence exemption (Summit County Ordinance
787-A).”
3. Staff shall review and approve all final landscaping plans for the site; including plant
type, size, number, location and method of irrigation and verify that they are in
compliance with the Canyons Development Agreement and the Snyderville Basin
Development Code.  Existing mature vegetation along the southern boundary should be
protected as much as possible during construction.
4. The Staff shall review and approve the lighting plan for the site; including building,
parking area, and street fixture type, number, location and cut sheets, and photometric
information for complete compliance with Section 4.22 of the Code, prior to issuance of
a building permit.
5. A detailed Development Improvement Agreement (DIA) shall be submitted to and
approved by the County Engineer, along with sufficient bonding for all infrastructure
improvements, common area improvements, landscaping and re -vegetation. This
agreement shall be recorded concurrently with the final site plan, prior to issuance of
any development permits.  All necessary construction plan and inspection fees must be
paid to the County Engineer prior to issuance of any permits.
6. All service provider conditions shall be met to the provider’s satisfaction, prior to
issuance of any development permits.
7. The Vintage on the Stand Phase 3 Final Site Plan for the must be approved and recorded
prior to issuance of any development permits.
8. All outstanding DRC items shall be completed prior to final approval of the Final Site
Plan or prior to the issuance of any development permits, as outlined in the DRC
recommendation letter.

GRADING AND EROSION CONTROL NOTES
1. CONTROLLING SEDIMENT TRANSPORT AND PREVENTING AND/OR CORRECTING
PROBLEMS ASSOCIATED WITH EROSION AND RUNOFF PROCESSES WHICH COULD OCCUR
BOTH DURING AND AFTER PROJECT CONSTRUCTION WILL BE CLOSELY MONITORED.
PERIODIC MAINTENANCE AND INSPECTION OF SEDIMENT CONTROL DEVICES WILL BE
SCHEDULED. PARTICULAR ATTENTION SHALL BE GIVEN TO EXISTING DRAINAGE PATTERNS
WHICH RUN THROUGH DISTURBED AREAS AND OVER EXTREME SLOPES. THESE PATTERNS
WILL BE IDENTIFIED TO ISOLATE PROBLEM AREAS WHERE WATER WILL CONCENTRATE.
PROVISIONS SHALL BE MADE TO CHANNEL RUNOFF AWAY FROM NEW OR EXISTING
IMPROVEMENTS TO PREVENT UNDERMINING AND GENERAL SITE EROSION. THESE
PROVISIONS SHALL BE STABILIZED AND SHALL REMAIN IN PLACE UNTIL THE PERMANENT
STORM DRAINAGE FACILITIES ARE INSTALLED AND FUNCTIONAL.
2. BEFORE CONSTRUCTION BEGINS, THE LIMITS OF DISTURBANCE BOUNDARY SHALL BE
FLAGGED ON SITE AND APPROVED BY COUNTY REPRESENTATIVE AND THE ENGINEER.
UNDER NO CIRCUMSTANCES SHALL SITE DISTURBANCE OCCUR OUTSIDE THE DESIGNATED
AREAS AT ANY TIME DURING CONSTRUCTION.
3. EXCAVATION AND EMBANKMENT OPERATIONS SHALL PROCEED IN SUCH A MANNER SO
THAT FINISHING OF SLOPES, INCLUDING REVEGETATION, SHALL BE ACCOMPLISHED AS
SOON AS POSSIBLE AFTER ROUGH GRADING. ALL SLOPES 2:1 OR FLATTER SHALL BE
SCARIFIED WITH HEAVY EQUIPMENT, LEAVING TRACKS PERPENDICULAR TO THE SLOPE,
4. ALL PUBLIC ROADWAYS MUST BE CLEARED DAILY OF ALL DIRT, MUD AND DEBRIS
DEPOSITED ON THEM AS A RESULT OF THE GRADING OPERATION AND PERFORMED TO THE
SATISFACTION OF THE COUNTY ENGINEER.
5. DISTURBED AREAS, BOTH ON AND OFF-SITE SHALL BE REVEGETATED. THESE AREAS
SHALL INCLUDE, BUT NOT BE LIMITED TO ALL UNSURFACED AREAS WITHIN THE FLAGGED
LIMITS OF DISTURBANCE, STAGING AND STORAGE AREAS, MATERIAL WASTE AREAS,
UNDERGROUND UTILITY CONSTRUCTION AREAS, BENCHED AREAS INCLUDING RETAINING
WALL BENCHES, AND TEMPORARY OR EXISTING ACCESS ROADS USED FOR
CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITIES.
6. A SWALE SEDIMENT TRAP FOR ALL DRAINAGEWAYS INTERCEPTED BY PROPOSED ROAD
CONSTRUCTION WILL BE FORMED. TRAPS WILL BE PLACED IN SURFACE DRAIN DITCHES
JUST BEFORE THE RUNOFF WATER LEAVES THE PROPERTY, ENTERS A WATERCOURSE OR
IMMEDIATELY PRECEDING DITCH INLETS OR STABILIZED OUTLETS.
7. SURFACE WATER SHALL DRAIN AWAY FROM THE STRUCTURES AT ALL POINTS.
GRADING ADJACENT TO ALL STRUCTURES SHALL FALL A MINIMUM OF 6" WITHIN THE FIRST
10'. ALL PATIOS SHALL HAVE A MINIMUM OF 2% SLOPE AWAY FROM THE STRUCTURE.
8. FOUNDATION WALLS SHALL EXTEND A MINIMUM OF 6" ABOVE ALL ADJACENT FINSHED
GRADES.
9. THE SITE SHALL BE GRADED TO DIRECT COLLECTED RUNOFF THROUGH SILT FENCES
OR STRAW BALES.
10. SILT FENCE SHALL BE INSTALLED AT THE TOE OF ALL DOWNHILL SLOPES IN THE
CONSTRUCTION AREAS. NO GRUBBED AREA SHALL BE WITHOUT SILT FENCEOR OTHER
EROSION CONTROL DEVICE FOR LONGER THAN 48 HOURS. ALL SILT FENCE OR L.O.D.
FENCING IS TO BE REPAIRED/REPLACED AS NECESSARY.
11. SEDIMENT TRAPS MUST OUTLET ONTO STABILIZED (PREFERABLY UNDISTURBED)
GROUND, INTO A WATER COURSE, OR (IN THE CASE OF A SMALL DRAINAGEWAY) LEFT TO
PERCOLATE INTO THE GROUND.
12. ALL SEED MIX SHALL BE APPLIED AT A RATE SO THAT GERMINATION AND SUBSEQUENT
COVERAGE REACHES 80 PERCENT IN A REPRESENTATIVE 10' BY 10' AREA. IF COVERAGE
DOES NOT REACH 80 PERCENT, RESEEDING MUST OCCUR.
13. FUGITIVE DUST SHALL BE CONTROLLED BY WATERING AND/OR CHEMICAL
STABALIZATION, PROVIDING VEGETATIVE OR SYNTHETIC COVER AND WIND BREAKS
CONSISTENT WITH UTAH STATE DIVISION OF AIR QUALITY STANDARDS.

FLOOR PLAN LEGEND
HATCH PATTERN DESCRIPTION
1. ALL DIMENSIONS ARE TO INTERIOR FACE-OF-STUD (F.O.S.) UNLESS NOTED OTHERWISE.
2. CEILING HEIGHTS MEASURED FROM PLYWOOD OR CONCRETE - SEE SECTIONS
3. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL UNIT DIMENSIONS, WINDOW TYPES, DOORS AND WALLS.
4. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL DECKS/PATIOS.
5. COORDINATE WITH ALL ENLARGED PLANS FOR ADDITIONAL INFORMATION AND DETAILS.
6. ALL TOPPING SLABS MUST BE POURED AFTER ROOF IS COMPLETE AND BUILDING IS DRIED IN.
7. SEE SHEET A002 FOR PROJECT GENERAL NOTES AND SHEET A003 FOR PROJECT KEYNOTES.  REVIEW ALL NOTES PRIOR 
TO CONSTRUCTION.
8. COORDINATE WITH STRUCTURAL FRAMING PLANS AND SHEAR WALL PLANS FOR LOCATIONS OF COLUMNS, BEAMS, 
SHEAR WALLS, ETC.
9. COORDINATE WITH BUILDER/OWNER FOR ALL INTERIOR FINISHES
10.  COORDINATE WITH ELECTRICAL DRAWINGS FOR ALL LIGHTING, POWER AND DATA REQUIREMENTS.
11. ALL EXTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X6 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
12.  ALL INTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X4 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
13.  ALL ROOF TRUSSES TO HAVE RAISED ENERGY HEEL CONSTRUCTION TO ALLOW FOR FULL DEPTH INSULATION OVER 
EXTERIOR WALLS (COORDINATE INSULATION REQUIREMENTS WITH RESCHECKS).

8. COORDINATE WITH STRUCTURAL FRAMING PLANS AND SHEAR WALL PLANS FOR LOCATIONS OF COLUMNS, BEAMS, 
SHEAR WALLS, ETC.
9. COORDINATE WITH BUILDER/OWNER FOR ALL INTERIOR FINISHES
10.  COORDINATE WITH ELECTRICAL DRAWINGS FOR ALL LIGHTING, POWER AND DATA REQUIREMENTS.
11. ALL EXTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X6 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
12.  ALL INTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X4 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
13.  ALL ROOF TRUSSES TO HAVE RAISED ENERGY HEEL CONSTRUCTION TO ALLOW FOR FULL DEPTH INSULATION OVER 
EXTERIOR WALLS (COORDINATE INSULATION REQUIREMENTS WITH RESCHECKS).

1. ALL DIMENSIONS ARE TO INTERIOR FACE-OF-STUD (F.O.S.) UNLESS NOTED OTHERWISE.
2. CEILING HEIGHTS MEASURED FROM PLYWOOD OR CONCRETE - SEE SECTIONS
3. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL UNIT DIMENSIONS, WINDOW TYPES, DOORS AND WALLS.
4. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL DECKS/PATIOS.
5. COORDINATE WITH ALL ENLARGED PLANS FOR ADDITIONAL INFORMATION AND DETAILS.
6. ALL TOPPING SLABS MUST BE POURED AFTER ROOF IS COMPLETE AND BUILDING IS DRIED IN.
7. SEE SHEET A0.1-A0.2 FOR PROJECT GENERAL NOTES AND SHEET A0.3-A0.7 FOR PROJECT KEYNOTES.  REVIEW ALL 
NOTES PRIOR TO CONSTRUCTION.
8. COORDINATE WITH STRUCTURAL FRAMING PLANS AND SHEAR WALL PLANS FOR LOCATIONS OF COLUMNS, BEAMS, 
SHEAR WALLS, ETC.
9. COORDINATE WITH BUILDER/OWNER FOR ALL INTERIOR FINISHES
10.  COORDINATE WITH ELECTRICAL DRAWINGS FOR ALL LIGHTING, POWER AND DATA REQUIREMENTS.
11. ALL EXTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X6 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
12.  ALL INTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X4 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
13.  ALL ROOF TRUSSES TO HAVE RAISED ENERGY HEEL CONSTRUCTION TO ALLOW FOR FULL DEPTH INSULATION OVER 
EXTERIOR WALLS (COORDINATE INSULATION REQUIREMENTS WITH RESCHECKS).

13.  ALL ROOF TRUSSES TO HAVE RAISED ENERGY HEEL CONSTRUCTION TO ALLOW FOR FULL DEPTH INSULATION OVER 
EXTERIOR WALLS (COORDINATE INSULATION REQUIREMENTS WITH RESCHECKS).

FLOOR PLAN LEGEND
HATCH PATTERN DESCRIPTION
1. ALL DIMENSIONS ARE TO INTERIOR FACE-OF-STUD (F.O.S.) UNLESS NOTED OTHERWISE.
2. CEILING HEIGHTS MEASURED FROM PLYWOOD OR CONCRETE - SEE SECTIONS
3. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL UNIT DIMENSIONS, WINDOW TYPES, DOORS AND WALLS.
4. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL DECKS/PATIOS.
5. COORDINATE WITH ALL ENLARGED PLANS FOR ADDITIONAL INFORMATION AND DETAILS.
6. ALL TOPPING SLABS MUST BE POURED AFTER ROOF IS COMPLETE AND BUILDING IS DRIED IN.
7. SEE SHEET A0.1-A0.2 FOR PROJECT GENERAL NOTES AND SHEET A0.3-A0.7 FOR PROJECT KEYNOTES.  REVIEW ALL 
NOTES PRIOR TO CONSTRUCTION.
8. COORDINATE WITH STRUCTURAL FRAMING PLANS AND SHEAR WALL PLANS FOR LOCATIONS OF COLUMNS, BEAMS, 
SHEAR WALLS, ETC.
9. COORDINATE WITH BUILDER/OWNER FOR ALL INTERIOR FINISHES
10.  COORDINATE WITH ELECTRICAL DRAWINGS FOR ALL LIGHTING, POWER AND DATA REQUIREMENTS.
11. ALL EXTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X6 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
12.  ALL INTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X4 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
13.  ALL ROOF TRUSSES TO HAVE RAISED ENERGY HEEL CONSTRUCTION TO ALLOW FOR FULL DEPTH INSULATION OVER 
EXTERIOR WALLS (COORDINATE INSULATION REQUIREMENTS WITH RESCHECKS).

HATCH PATTERN DESCRIPTION
1. ALL DIMENSIONS ARE TO INTERIOR FACE-OF-STUD (F.O.S.) UNLESS NOTED OTHERWISE.
2. CEILING HEIGHTS MEASURED FROM PLYWOOD OR CONCRETE - SEE SECTIONS
3. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL UNIT DIMENSIONS, WINDOW TYPES, DOORS AND WALLS.
4. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL DECKS/PATIOS.
5. COORDINATE WITH ALL ENLARGED PLANS FOR ADDITIONAL INFORMATION AND DETAILS.
6. ALL TOPPING SLABS MUST BE POURED AFTER ROOF IS COMPLETE AND BUILDING IS DRIED IN.
7. SEE SHEET A0.1-A0.2 FOR PROJECT GENERAL NOTES AND SHEET A0.4-A0.7 FOR PROJECT KEYNOTES.  REVIEW ALL 
NOTES PRIOR TO CONSTRUCTION.
8. COORDINATE WITH STRUCTURAL FRAMING PLANS AND SHEAR WALL PLANS FOR LOCATIONS OF COLUMNS, BEAMS, 
SHEAR WALLS, ETC.
9. COORDINATE WITH BUILDER/OWNER FOR ALL INTERIOR FINISHES
10.  COORDINATE WITH ELECTRICAL DRAWINGS FOR ALL LIGHTING, POWER AND DATA REQUIREMENTS.
11. ALL EXTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X6 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
12.  ALL INTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X4 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
13.  ALL ROOF TRUSSES TO HAVE RAISED ENERGY HEEL CONSTRUCTION TO ALLOW FOR FULL DEPTH INSULATION OVER 
EXTERIOR WALLS (COORDINATE INSULATION REQUIREMENTS WITH RESCHECKS).

FLOOR PLAN LEGEND
HATCH PATTERN DESCRIPTION
1. ALL DIMENSIONS ARE TO INTERIOR FACE-OF-STUD (F.O.S.) UNLESS NOTED OTHERWISE.
2. CEILING HEIGHTS MEASURED FROM PLYWOOD OR CONCRETE - SEE SECTIONS
3. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL UNIT DIMENSIONS, WINDOW TYPES, DOORS AND WALLS.
4. REFER TO ENLARGED PLANS FOR ALL DECKS/PATIOS.
5. COORDINATE WITH ALL ENLARGED PLANS FOR ADDITIONAL INFORMATION AND DETAILS.
6. ALL TOPPING SLABS MUST BE POURED AFTER ROOF IS COMPLETE AND BUILDING IS DRIED IN.
7. SEE SHEET A0.1-A0.2 FOR PROJECT GENERAL NOTES AND SHEET A0.4-A0.7 FOR PROJECT KEYNOTES.  REVIEW ALL 
NOTES PRIOR TO CONSTRUCTION.
8. COORDINATE WITH STRUCTURAL FRAMING PLANS AND SHEAR WALL PLANS FOR LOCATIONS OF COLUMNS, BEAMS, 
SHEAR WALLS, ETC.
9. COORDINATE WITH BUILDER/OWNER FOR ALL INTERIOR FINISHES
10.  COORDINATE WITH ELECTRICAL DRAWINGS FOR ALL LIGHTING, POWER AND DATA REQUIREMENTS.
11. ALL EXTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X6 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
12.  ALL INTERIOR WALLS ARE ASSUMED TO BE 2X4 STUD WALLS UNLESS SHOWN/NOTED OTHERWISE.
13.  ALL ROOF TRUSSES TO HAVE RAISED ENERGY HEEL CONSTRUCTION TO ALLOW FOR FULL DEPTH INSULATION OVER 
EXTERIOR WALLS (COORDINATE INSULATION REQUIREMENTS WITH RESCHECKS).

INDINGS OF FACT & 
CONCLUSIONS OF LAW 
O
ctober 26, 2017 
T
HIS MATTER came before the Summit County Manager (the “Manager”) on May 4, 
2017, and October 23, 2017 on Sugarbowl Developers, LC (the “Developer”) request for 
approval of its Vintage on the Strand, Phase III Final Site Plan and Condominium Plat (the 
“Phase III Plat”) pursuant to Section 2.7 of the Amended and Restated Development Agreement 
for the Canyons Specially Planned Area, dated November 15, 1999, as amended (the 
“Development Agreement”).   
Walter Plumb, Sam Ferrin and Ron Ferrin represented the Developer.  The Department of 
Community Development was represented by Patrick Putt, Community Development Director, 
and Tiffanie Robinson, County Planner.  The Manager was represented by David L. Thomas, 
Chief Civil Deputy County Attorney. 
EXHIBIT C1
108
 
2 
Evidence and materials were presented by way of testimony, statements, documents and 
memorandum for consideration by the Manager.  Having considered the evidence presented by 
all interested parties and the entire record relating to this matter, the Manager rendered his 
decision, after holding a properly noticed public hearing, as part of his regularly scheduled 
agenda on October 26, 2017, finding that Developer has conditionally satisfied the requirements 
of the Development Agreement for approval of the Phase III Plat.   In support of that decision, 
the Manager adopts the following Findings of Fact, Conclusions of Law and Conditions: 
 FINDINGS OF FACT  
1. On November 15, 1999, Summit County (the “County”) entered into a D evelopment 
Agreement with various property owners within the Canyons Specially Planned Area.1 
2. The Development Agreement provided for the uses, densities and configurations for the 
Canyons Resort.  Of import are Exhibit A to the Development Agreement (“Statement of Global 
Principles”)
2 and Exhibit B (“Land Use & Zoning Chart”).3  The Statement of Global Principles 
is not part of the preamble to the Development Agreement, but is incorporated into the 
substantive regulations in Finding 13 and is expressly defined in Article 1, wherein it states: 
Statement of Global Principles” means those mandatory development principles 
and standards established in The Canyons SPA Plan, attached as Exhibit A.1 
hereto, which are in addition to the development standards delineated in the Code 
and General Plan, which shall be used to guide all development within The 
 
1 Exhibit C (Amended and Restated Development Agreement for the Canyons Specially Planned Area, dated 
November 15, 1999 (“Development Agreement”)). 
 
2 Exhibit A (“Statement of Global Principles”). 
 
3 Exhibit B (“Land Use & Zoning Chart”). 
 
EXHIBIT C2
109
 
3 
Canyons SPA and which shall apply, as described herein, to both Project Sites 
within The Canyons SPA and to all amendments to The Canyons SPA and SPA 
Plan.4  (emphasis added) 
 
3. The Development Agreement expressly requires that the County’s approval of any 
subdivision plat, condominium plat or site plan be “based upon the Developer’s compliance with 
the policies of the General Plan and the requirements and standards set forth in the Code and in 
this Amended Agreement.”
5  The approval of site plans and plats are administrative acts 
approved by the County executive. 
4. Global Principle 3 (Required Unit Configurations and Occupancy for all development in 
the Canyons SPA) discusses in detail the importance of “destination accommodations” and 
“hotel/lodging” within the Resort Core, including what constitutes such for purposes of the 
Development Agreement. 
The Canyons Resort Center differs from a Town Center in that there shall be a 
focus on destination accommodations rather than primary residential dwelling 
units. . . . The General Plan requires that development must be oriented largely to 
resort and guest accommodations that support the recreational nature of the area.  
. . .  For the purpose of the Canyons SPA, “resort and guest accommodations” 
shall include those units in which all beds are occupied on a temporary short-term 
basis, and not occupied as full-time primary residential dwelling units.  The 
primary purpose of this type of unit shall be to provide short-term 
accommodations for owners and non-owners using the resort facilities. . . . 
Multiple family dwelling units where there is condominium ownership and 
operation in the form of a hotel or lodge (condo hotel) as described above shall be 
considered resort and guest accommodations only in the Resort Core, unless 
otherwise approved by Summit County. . . . Within the Resort Core . . . the 
targeted bed allocation shall be at least ninety percent (90%) resort and guest 
 
4 Exhibit C (Development Agreement, Article 1). 
 
5 Exhibit C (Development Agreement §2.7.8). 
 
EXHIBIT C3
110
 
4 
accommodations.  There shall be no more than ten percent (10%) of the beds 
allocated to primary dwelling units in the Resort Core.6  (emphasis added). 
 
5. Within the Resort Core neighborhood, parcels designated as Friedman 1 (“F1”) and 
Friedman 2 (“F2”), are owned by Developer.7  Hence, the Friedman parcels have been in 
substantially the same ownership for the entire duration of the Development Agreement.8 
6. According to the Land Use & Zoning Chart, the Friedman parcels’ uses, densities and 
configuration are as follows:9

7. “Hotel/Lodging Units” is an expressly defined term within the Development Agreement. 
Hotel/Lodging Unit(s) means a unit which shall contain attributes of a hotel o[r] 
facility established for similar purposes and which shall be available for short 
term occupancy by the unit owner or others.  Attributes shall include: 
 
Central reservation service for all units, including central check-in with 
full-time front desk service, bellhops, and concierge, operated by the 
owner/operator, a property management company chosen by the owners’ 
association, or as a function of the owner’s association [sic]; 
 
Central access to the building, with no private entrances for individual

include up to but not to exceed four dwelling units, unless otherwise

8. Amendments to the permitted uses within the Land Use & Zoning Chart are categorized 
in the Development Agreement as Substantial Amendments.
11  Substantial Amendments are 
legislative acts.  The Manager has no authority to grant Substantial Amendments.

Plat (together, the “Phase I Plat”) was approved by the County Commission, sitting in their 
executive capacity (the “Phase I Approval”), and recorded on November 4, 2005. The Phase I 
Approval was for 66,374 square feet of density and Hotel/Lodging use.

10. The Phase I Plat consisted of 7 multi-family and 2 single family dwelling structures.14   
11. As illustrated by the minutes to the County Commission’s Phase I Approval of July 20, 
2005, Commissioner Richer voiced his concern that “the applicant’s parcels, which were 
designated as hotel lodging/retail, are being turned into multi-family units and that multi-family 
units elsewhere have morphed into primary residences.”
15   The overriding intent that the Phase I 
Approval reflect Hotel/Lodging uses was echoed at the time by planning staff and the 
Snyderville Basin Planning Commission.16 
12. The County Commission conditioned the P hase I Approval to ensure Hotel/Lodging Unit

the intended lodging use, staff suggests that language be added to the plat in the form of a note and to the CCR’s for 
the project clarifying that none of the units in the project are eligible for primary residence status. . . .” (Exhibit I 
(Staff Report, dated July 8, 2005)).

• The Phase III Application is restricted to Hotel/Lodging Units. 
• The definition of Hotel/Lodging Unit within the Development Agreement controls. 
• So long as the parameters set out in the definition of Hotel/Lodging Unit are satisfied, 
units designed as either single family or multi-family structures can be compliant with the 
definition of Hotel/Lodging Unit. 
• The Development Agreement restricts permanent residences within the Resort Core and

19.  At the May 4, 2017 public hearing, the discussion centered around compliance with two 
specific components of the Hotel/Lodging Unit definition: 
 a. Central reservation service for all units, including central check-in with full-time 
front desk service, bellhops, and concierge; and 
b. Utilities centrally controlled, including cable television, telephone, electricity, gas 
and water.  
   20. The Manager continued the public hearing on May 4, 2017, to allow the Developer to 
supplement its evidence to show its compliance with the Hotel/Lodging Unit definition.  
  21. On October 23, 2017, Developer provided evidence that: 
  a. It would convert a structure on the property to a central reservation and check-in 
with full-time desk service, bellhops and concierge; and 
  b. It would comply with any requirements of Rocky Mountain Power, Dominion 
Energy, and Summit Water Distribution Company for centralized utilities.   
 BASED on the totality of facts and circumstances presented by the evidence and the 
entire record considered as part of the decision, the Manager  renders the following Conclusions 
of Law: 
 
26 Exhibit O (Advisory Opinion, Office of the Property Rights Ombudsman, November 23, 2016). 
 
EXHIBIT C9

10 
 CONCLUSIONS OF LAW 
1. A landowner vests its uses and densities upon the filing of a completed development 
application which conforms to the land use laws then in effect.27   
2. The Phase I Approval vested all density and uses pertaining to 66,374 square feet in 
Hotel/Lodging Units consistent with the Land Use & Zoning Chart.    
3. In making the Phase I Approval, the County Commission placed conditions upon those 
units: (a) prohibiting their qualification as primary residences, and (b) requiring them to be 
placed into a rental pool.
28  These conditions are consistent with Hotel/Lodging Units, as defined 
in the Development Agreement, and Global Principle 3.29  These conditions appear in the 
Hotel/Lodging Plat Note. 
4. The greyscape illustrative Parcels 2A and 2B within the Phase I Plat (inclusive of the 
Final Site Plan) together with Plat Note #10, which unambiguously provides that the future Lots 
are not yet created, demonstrate that the Phase I Approval was limited in scope to the 66,374 
square feet of density.  Otherwise, there would have been no need for the greyscape or Plat Note 
#10.
30  The greyscape was illustrative in nature and meant to provide a pictorial representation of 
 
27 UCA §17-27a-508; Western Land Equities v. Logan, 617 P.2d 388 (Utah 1980).   
  
28 Exhibit F (Summit County Commission meeting minutes, dated July 20, 2005). 
 
29 Exhibit C (Development Agreement, Article 1); Exhibit A (“Statement of Global Principles,” Global Principle 3). 
 
30 Rogers v. West Valley City, 142 P.3d 554 (Utah App. 2006) (interpretation of zoning ordinances is guided by 
standard rules of statutory construction); LeBeau v. State, 2014 UT 39, ¶20, 337 P.3d 254, 260 (Utah 2014) 
(discussing statutory interpretation techniques); Marion Energy, Inc. v. KFJ Ranch Partnership, 2011 UT 50, ¶ 14, 
267 P.3d 863, 866 (Utah 2011) (courts “presume[] that the expression of one [term] should be interpreted as the 
exclusion of another”); Selvig v. Blockbuster Enterss, LC, 266 P.3d 691, 696 (Utah 2011)(“In interpreting a

the entire site.  Such is commonly done within the land use planning field, but does not rise to 
the level of a vested development right.  Consequently, the Hotel/Lodging Plat Note is limited to 
Phase I and the Manager will not disturb that approval.

5. With respect to the Phase III Application, the remaining 52,800 square feet of density and

requirements of Hotel/Lodging Units, so long as the definitional provisions of a Hotel/Lodging 
 
contract, we look to the writing itself to ascertain the parties’ intentions, and we consider each provision . . . in 
relation to all of the others, with a view toward giving effect to all and ignoring none.”) . 
  
31 The fact that Developer has substantially altered the site plan, including the lot lines, in the Phase III Application 
from the Phase I Plat is further evidence that the Developer never relied upon a vested right to the greyscape 
illustrative Parcels 2A and 2B, otherwise such would not have been modified.  The purpose of Plat Note #4 was to 
allow the Developer to amend the plat without requiring the consent of the other lot owners.  While prior to 2005, 
such unanimous consent of the lot owners was required by statute, such was modified in 2005 to no longer require 
unanimity.  UCA §17-27a-609(4)(b).  In sum, Plat Note #4 still required an amendment to the plat to go through the 
statutorily required process, inclusive of land use authority approval , for any changes to the Phase I Plat.  UCA §17-
27a-608.

8. Developer was given the opportunity to supplement its evidence to satisfy the 
requirements for Hotel/Lodging Units, specifically: 
• Central reservation service for all units, including central check-in with full-time front 
desk service, bellhops, and concierge; and 
•  Utilities centrally controlled, including cable television, telephone, electricity, gas and 
water. 
9. On October 23, 2017, the Developer adequately demonstrated compliance with the 
requirements of Hotel/Lodging Units with respect to the Phase III Plat.   
10. The Phase III Plat is approved, subject to the following conditions:  
CONDITIONS 
1. Submission to the Department of Community Development of an updated Canyons DRC 
recommendation letter that includes stamped and approved drawings of the proposed Managers 
Unit/Rental Office. 
2. Planning Staff shall review and approve all final landscaping plans for the site, including 
plant type, size, number, location and method of irrigation, and verify that they are in compliance 
with the Development Agreement and Chapter 10 of the Summit County Code. 
 
35 Exhibit O (Advisory Opinion, Office of the Property Rights Ombudsman, November 23, 2016). 
 
EXHIBIT C12
119
 
13 
3. Planning Staff shall review and approve the lighting plan for the site, including building, 
parking area, and street fixture type, number, location and cut sheets, and the photometric 
information, for complete compliance with §10-4-21 of the Summit County Code. 
4. Planning Staff shall review and approve any signage associated with the site prior to 
installation. 
5. A detailed Development Improvements Agreement (“DIA”) shall be submitted to the 
County Engineer and approved, along with sufficient bonding for all infrastructure 
improvements, common area improvements, landscaping and re-vegetation.  This must also 
clearly denote the phasing plan for the project and any triggers for landscaping and re-vegetation 
if the construction does commence on the phases within the specific allotted time frame.  The 
DIA shall be recorded concurrently with the final site plan and Phase III Plat prior to issuance of 
any development permits.  All necessary construction plans and inspection fees must be paid to 
the County Engineer prior to issuance of any development permits. 
6. All service provider conditions shall be met to the provider’s satisfaction prior to the 
issuance of any development permits. 
7. Electrical, Water and Gas utilities must be centrally located and not individually metered.

14 
8. The Summit County Attorney’s Office shall review and approve the final site plan and 
Phase III Plat notes and review the CC&Rs for consistency with the conditions of approval and 
terms of the Development Agreement. 
9. All outstanding Canyons DRC items shall be completed prior to issuance of any 
development permits, as outlined in the Canyons DRC recommendation letter. 
 This land use decision is appealable to the Summit County Council pursuant to Summit 
County Code §10-9-22(A).

36 Exhibit C.  Development Agreement §2.7.10 provides all appeal rights pertaining to the approval of development

EXHIBIT C15
122
“Hotel/Loding Unit(s) means a unit which shall contain attributes of a hotel or facility established 
for similar purposes and which shall be available for short term occupancy by the unit owner or 
others. Attributes shall include”: - Confirmed.  The project is part of the Vintage on the Strand 
Condominiums, and will be operated similarly to the existing condo units – in other words, 
condominium owners will have access to the central reservation system to rent each unit for short 
term occupancy, owners and guests will check-in through the Rental Office, and a note has been 
placed on the Condominium Plat and Final Site Plan stating – “The Principle Use shall be as 
Hotel/Lodging units, and as such are not eligible for the primary residence exception (Summit 
County Ordinance 787-A). ”  
i. Central reservation service for all units, including central check-in with full-
time front desk service, bellhops, and concierge, operated by the
owner/operator, a property management company chosen by the owners’ 
association, or as a function of the owners’ association; - Confirmed. A
Rental Office will be constructed as part of the project to ensure that each
condominium unit is operated as a Hotel/Lodging unit.
ii. Central access to the building, with no private entrances for individual units
or wings, except in structures which include up to but not to exceed four
dwelling units, unless otherwise approved by the Director; - Confirmed. Each
structure is an individual condominium unit and check-in is at the Rental
Office.
iii. Pedestrian traffic funneled through a central lobby area, except in structures
which include up to but not to exceed four dwelling units, unless otherwise
approved by the Director; - Confirmed. Each structure is an individual
condominium unit and check-in is at the Rental Office.
iv. Centralized parking, with no assigned spaces, except in structures which
include up to but not to exceed four dwelling units, unless otherwise
approved by the Director; - Confirmed. Each structure is an individual condo
unit with a two-car garage.
v. Utilities centrally controlled, including cable television, telephone,
electricity, gas and water; and – Confirmed. Each major utility has provided a
will-serve letter and utilities are controlled by and through the Condominium
HOA.
vi. Limited storage area for owners. – Confirmed. Each condominium unit will
have limited owner storage.

February 12, 2026
Dear Developer: 
Re: Natural Gas Service Availability Letter 
Natural gas can be made available to serve Vintage 3 development when the 
following requirements are met: 
1. Developer provides plat maps, drawings, construction schedules and/or
buildings that will be served by natural gas, and all other relevant information
regarding commercial and residential uses, including but not limited to,
proposed natural gas appliances (number and type of appliances per unit,
homes, building).
2. Review by Enbridge Gas' Engineering and/or Pre-Construction Department to
determine load requirements. System reinforcement requirements and
estimated costs to bring natural gas to the development.
Upon completion of Enbridge Gas' review of the development's natural gas requirements, 
agreements will be prepared, as necessary, for high pressure, intermediate high pressure 
and/or service line extensions required to serve the development. These service 
extensions must be paid in advance. 
To accommodate your construction schedule and provide cost estimates to you, 
please contact me at your earliest convenience. 
Sincerely, 
Whit Sargent 
Pre-Construction Representative

EXHIBIT E5
128
SUSTAINABILITY STATEMENT 
The overall goals for sustainability at the Canyons Resort as outlined in the SPA are: 
• Reduced energy consumption
• Use of locally sourced and renewable materials
• Increased recycling efforts
• Encourage water efficiency
Good sustainability practices involve designing, constructing, and operating buildings that 
minimize environmental impact through high energy efficiency, water conservation, 
sustainable materials, recycling efforts and healthy indoor air quality. 
Vintage on the Strand – Phase 3 Condominiums will work to attain these sustainability 
goals by implementing and following standards outlined below in each of the categories. 
Reduced energy consumption: 
• Provide high-efficiency Energy Star rated appliances and smart thermostats to
reduce daily power consumption
• Desing buildings that utilize the use of natural light and provide shaded and sunny
outdoor areas to manage solar gain
• Utilize high-quality insulation (e.g., spray foam), triple paned windows, and seal air
leaks to reduce heating/cooling demands which produces higher thermal control of
the building enclosure
• Provide LED and dark sky compliant lighting
EXHIBIT F1
129
• Ensuring healthy air and indoor environmental quality through proper ventilation 
and using effective HVAC equipment, use of formaldehyde-free finishes, and 
products with minimum off-gassing or low volatile organic compounds 
• Use of techniques that emphasize natural ventilation of indoor spaces 
 
Use of locally sourced and renewable materials: 
• Purchase local or regionally produced building materials 
• Provide materials that will stand the effects of summer and winter environments to 
reduce the need for frequent replacement or maintenance 
• Use of Certified wood and building materials that are sustainably harvested and 
manufactured 
• Choose materials with low "embodied carbon" (the energy used to produce and 
transport them) 
• Rocks harvested on site 
 
Increased recycling efforts: 
• Use of high-performance engineered wood, wood alternatives, allergen-free 
materials, recycled building materials and sustainably harvested lumber  
• Reducing, reusing, and recycling construction waste, and employing prefab 
elements to minimize on-site waste 
• Use of Construction Waste Management Protocols 
• Selecting mechanical equipment based on life-cycle costs (last longer) 
• Providing buildings that require fewer repairs over their lifetime is inherently more 
sustainable than one that needs frequent replacement 
 
Encourage water efficiency: 
• Plant native, low maintenance/non-water intensive vegetation 
• Provide drip irrigation as much as possible 
• Install low-flow toilets, showerheads and faucets

R
E: Summary Letter of Recommendation - Vintage on the Strand Phase III 
M
rs. Northrup-Robinson, 
CVMA has reviewed the request from the Vintage on The Strand developer to renew our approval letter to move forward 
with phase 3 of the development. We have reviewed the submittal, and the plans are in line with the original set of 
approved plans. You will find the plans reviewed by CVMA stamped and attached with this letter. The same conditions 
from the November 8, 2019 letter remain below: 
1) P
rior to the issuance of a Certificate of Occupancy, the CVMA must review and approve of the proposed
community CC&Rs. CC&Rs must reflect language regarding the CVMA assessments specific to billing, collection,
and remittance procedures.
2) A copy of the proposed County Development Improvement Agreement must be provided to the CVMA prior to
the issuance of a building permit.
3) Prior to the issuance of a building permit, the developer and appointed general contractor must meet with the
CVMA to discuss the proposed Construction Management and Mitigation Plan. This plan will include
construction staging, parking, laydown, SWPPP representative appointee, hours of operation, insurance, etc.
4) Prior to the issuance of a building permit, and in addition to the payment of applicable fees, a signed
Construction Mitigation & Management Agreement and a signed Construction Management & Mitigation
Performance Agreement must be submitted to the CVMA.
5) The developer will contact the CVMA in writing for a property inspection to be scheduled 30 days prior to the
issuance of any Certificate of Occupancy. As part of this inspection process, the applicant must fill and sign a
CVMA Density Verification Form for all units scheduled for inspection.
Please let me know if you have any questions. 
Thank you,  
On Behalf of the Design Review Committee 
Jo
hn Simmons 
Planning and Operations Manager 
Canyons Village Management Association, Inc.
